<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For every $`4/9\lt v\lt 1`$ and $`1\lt c\lt 9v/4`$, all sufficiently large integers <i>k</i> admit a complete smooth metric on ℝ<sup>3</sup> with nonnegative Ricci curvature, asymptotic volume ratio <i>v</i>, and at least $`c(k+1)^2`$ linearly independent real harmonic functions of pointwise growth at most <i>k</i>. This answers Yau's integer-degree dimension comparison question negatively in dimension three, with a fixed-factor excess over the Euclidean count. For each $`1\lt c\lt 9/4`$, these metrics can be chosen arbitrarily close to the Euclidean metric in global bi-Lipschitz distance. The metric may depend on <i>k</i>.

- **claim-main-dimension-three-counterexample** (theorem, [source](#stmt-n-main)): For every $v\in(4/9,1)$ and $c\in(1,9v/4)$, there is an integer $k_0(v,c)$ such that for every integer $k\geq k_0(v,c)$ there is a complete smooth metric $g_k$ on $\mathbb R^3$, Euclidean near the origin, with $\operatorname{Ric}_{g_k}\geq0$, $\operatorname{AVR}(g_k)=v$, and $$h_k(\mathbb R^3,g_k)\geq c(k+1)^2>(k+1)^2 =h_k(\mathbb R^3,g_{\mathrm E}).$$ The Ricci curvature is positive outside a compact set. The metric has uncountably many pairwise nonisometric pointed tangent cones at infinity. There are points tending to infinity and planes containing the radial direction at those points whose sectional curvature is negative. The metric is chosen after $k$ and may depend on it.
  - Explanation: For prescribed volume ratio and excess factor in the stated ranges, every large degree admits a complete metric on three-space with nonnegative Ricci curvature whose degree-k harmonic space is larger than the Euclidean one. This negatively answers the integer-degree comparison in dimension three. The metric is chosen after k.
- **claim-near-euclidean-bilipschitz** (theorem, [source](#stmt-n-near-euclidean)): For every $\epsilon>0$ and $1<c<9/4$, all sufficiently large integers $k$ admit metrics with the conclusions of Theorem <a href="#n:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="n:main">1</a> and $$(1+\epsilon)^{-2}g_{\mathrm E}\leq g_k \leq(1+\epsilon)^2g_{\mathrm E} \qquad\text{on }\mathbb R^3.$$ Their asymptotic volume ratio can be prescribed arbitrarily close to $1$. In particular, the identity map is globally $(1+\epsilon)$-bi-Lipschitz.
  - Explanation: The excess can be realised by metrics that are globally bi-Lipschitz close to the Euclidean metric, with volume ratio as close to one as desired.
- **lemma-two-splitting-directions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-n-lem-two-directions)): Let $\lambda>0$ be an eigenvalue of $h^0(w)$, and let $u,v$ be an orthonormal pair in its real eigenspace, using $L^2(d\mu_w)$. The traceless compression to $\operatorname{span}\{u,v\}$ of the first variation of the Laplacian has rank two as the smooth conformal direction varies. If $w$ is antipodally even and $u,v$ have the same antipodal parity, the conclusion still holds when only even conformal directions are allowed. The same splitting matrices apply after the fixed-area coordinate change <a href="#n:eq-fixed-area-gauge" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="n:eq-fixed-area-gauge">[n:eq-fixed-area-gauge]</a>.
  - Explanation: On any two-dimensional positive eigenspace of the conformal sphere family, the traceless part of the first variation of the Laplacian has rank two, so both splitting directions are available, also within the even family and after the fixed-area coordinate change.
- **lemma-finite-cutoff-avoidance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-n-lem-avoidance)): Parameters whose metrics are simple through any fixed position are dense in $\mathcal U$. Any two such parameters can be joined inside $\mathcal U$ by a smooth path that is simple through that position and is constant near its endpoints. In the even parameter family, the analogous statements hold for simplicity through prescribed finite cutoffs within each parity. Cross-parity coincidences are allowed in this last statement.
  - Explanation: Parameters whose spectrum is simple up to a fixed position are dense and can be joined by simple paths, also in the even family with a cutoff in each parity.
- **lemma-retained-isolated-double** (theorem, [source](#stmt-n-lem-retained-double)): Suppose that $h^0(w_*)$ has an isolated eigenvalue of exact multiplicity two at positions $i,i+1$. For any $q\geq i+1$ and any neighborhood of $w_*$ in $\mathcal U$, there is a parameter in that neighborhood for which the same positions are equal and all other inequalities through position $q$, including the gap after $q$, are strict. The perturbations here are unrestricted conformal perturbations, even when $w_*$ was obtained in the even family.
  - Explanation: A chosen isolated double eigenvalue can be kept exactly while all other coincidences through a finite cutoff are removed by small unrestricted conformal perturbations.
- **lemma-pole-sector-simplicity-parity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-n-lem-pole-modes)): Let $\zeta(\theta)>0$ be smooth as an axisymmetric function on $S^2$, and suppose $\zeta(\pi-\theta)=\zeta(\theta)$. In the metric $\zeta^2g_0$, fix a nonnegative integer azimuthal number $m$ and one azimuthal factor (the constant factor if $m=0$). Every eigenvalue of the meridional profile problem is simple. Its lowest profile can be chosen strictly positive on $(0,\pi)$ and is even under equatorial reflection. Along a smooth reflection-symmetric deformation, every fixed position in the $m=0$ list retains its reflection parity. In particular the position occupied at round by degree $l$ retains parity $(-1)^l$.
  - Explanation: For a reflection-symmetric axisymmetric conformal sphere, each azimuthal sector has simple profile eigenvalues, a positive even ground profile, and reflection parity that is constant along symmetric deformations.
- **lemma-adjacent-doubles** (theorem, [source](#stmt-n-lem-adjacent-doubles)): Fix $\vartheta>1$. For every prescribed sufficiently small neighborhood $\mathcal U$ as above, every sufficiently large integer $k$, and every $i$ with $B_L+1\leq i<p$, there is $w\in\mathcal U$ for which the only failure of simplicity through position $p+1$ is $\lambda_i(h(w))=\lambda_{i+1}(h(w))$. This eigenvalue has exact multiplicity two. In particular $\lambda_p(h(w))<\lambda_{p+1}(h(w))$.
  - Explanation: At every adjacent pair of positions in the band there is a metric whose only failure of simplicity through the outer cutoff is an isolated double at that pair, with the outer gap preserved. Pairs inside a round block use full splitting control; pairs across blocks use parity inversion.
- **prop-angular-program** (theorem, [source](#stmt-n-prop-angular-program)): Fix $2/3<a<1$, $1<\vartheta<3a/2$, and $a^2<\kappa_*<1$. Prescribe a sufficiently small conformal neighborhood $\mathcal U$ for which the curvature and comparison bounds above hold. For every sufficiently large integer $k$, with threshold allowed to depend on $\mathcal U$, there are $S>0$ and a smooth $S$-periodic path $w(s)$ in $\mathcal U$ such that $H(s)=h(w(s))$ has the following properties, with $M,p,L,I,N$ as in <a href="#n:eq-band" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="n:eq-band">[n:eq-band]</a>. The phase origin can be chosen so that $w(s)=w(0)$ for $\|s\|<\varepsilon_0$, for some $\varepsilon_0>0$, and $h(w(0))$ is simple through position $p+1$.* 1. *The area form is $d\operatorname{vol}_{H(s)}=4\pi\,d\mu$ pointwise, and the ordered outer gap $\lambda_p(H(s))<\lambda_{p+1}(H(s))$ holds for every $s$.* 2. *Along the unwrapped path $s\in\mathbb R$, the spectral subspace of the first $p$ positions has a smooth real $L^2(d\mu)$-orthonormal frame $e_1(s),\ldots,e_p(s)$ of continued eigenlines. Their only equalities are isolated double crossings at distinct phases, and at each crossing the traceless first derivative of its $2\times2$ cluster matrix is nonzero. The return permutation after $S$ fixes positions $1,\ldots,B_L$ and is one cycle on $I$.* 3. *With $$d(x)=\frac{-1+\sqrt{1+4x}}2,$$ every label among the first $p$ satisfies $$\label{n:eq-frequency-margin} \frac1{NS}\int_0^{NS}d\bigl(a^{-2}\lambda_i(s)\bigr)\,ds<k.$$ Here $NS$ is an eigenvalue period for every band label and also a period for every fixed lower label.* *At each crossing there is a smooth conformal direction $\xi$ for which the off-diagonal entry $$\left\langle e_h, \left.\frac{d}{d\varepsilon}\right\|_{\varepsilon=0} (-\Delta_{h(w+\varepsilon\xi)})e_i \right\rangle_{L^2(d\mu)}$$ is nonzero on the crossing pair. After $k$ and the entire program are fixed, the outer gap, the absolute crossing slopes, and the separations between crossing phases have positive uniform lower bounds. For every fixed order, the smooth norms of the frame and metrics have a uniform upper bound. For each pair, its absolute eigenvalue gap has a positive lower bound on every compact subset of the full label-period phase circle $\mathbb R/(NS\mathbb Z)$ disjoint from that pair’s crossing set. A pair with no crossings has a uniform gap on that whole circle.
  - Explanation: A periodic fixed-area path of near-round sphere metrics exists whose continued eigenlines have isolated linearly split double crossings, whose return permutation is a single cycle on the band, and whose every label has mean cone frequency strictly below k.
- **prop-uniform-radial-realization** (construction, [source](#stmt-n-prop-radial-realization)): Suppose the fixed reference program, including its negative-phase interpolation, is close enough to round that its Gauss curvature is uniformly greater than $\kappa_*$. There are a constant $C>0$ and, after $C$ is fixed, an integer $J_0$ such that for every integer $J\geq J_0$ there is a smooth function $f=f_J:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)$, independent of $z$, such that $$g_z=dr^2+f(r)^2H_z(\log r)$$ extends to a complete smooth metric on $\mathbb R^3$ for every $z\in[-1,1]^{\mathbb N}$. The metric is Euclidean near zero, $\operatorname{Ric}_{g_z}\geq0$, with strictly positive Ricci curvature outside a compact set, and $d_{g_z}(0,(r,x))=r$. Moreover $$a\leq f'(r)\leq1,\qquad f'(r)\to a,\qquad b(t):=e^{-t}f(e^t)\to a.$$ For every $t\geq0.8J$ the following estimates are uniform over the entire product box of controls, also after any fixed number of angular differentiations: $$\begin{aligned} &\partial_tH_z=O(\eta),\qquad \partial_t^2H_z=O(\eta^2), \qquad \operatorname{tr}(H_z^{-1}\partial_tH_z)=0, \label{n:angular-time-input}\\ &b_{tt}+b_t=-C\eta^2,\qquad b_t=O(C\eta^2),\quad b_{tt}=O(C\eta^2),\quad b-a=O(Ct^{-1/2}). \label{n:radial-tail-input} \end{aligned}$$ In every fixed smooth angular norm, $H_z(t)-H(s(t))=O(\eta(t)^{3/4})$ in this range, where the reference $H(s)=h(w(s))$ includes the negative-phase interpolation. The controlled links stay in a fixed compact subset of a finite-dimensional smooth family and have $K_{H_z}>\kappa_*$. All implied constants may depend on $a$, $\kappa_*$, the fixed angular program, and the fixed $C$; they do not depend on $J\geq J_0$ or on the control sequence.
  - Explanation: A single radial warping function, independent of the controls, turns every controlled angular path into a complete smooth metric on three-space with nonnegative Ricci curvature, positive outside a compact set, with uniform decay estimates.
- **lemma-inward-transfer-contraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-n-lem-inward-transfer)): For every control sequence, $\mathcal R^z_{r,R}$ is positivity preserving, fixes constants, preserves the $\mu$-mean, and is a contraction on $L^2(\mu)$ for every $0<r<R$. In particular these conclusions hold for $T_j$. Uniformly over all controls and all sufficiently late starts, $$\\|T_j-P_j^0\\|_{L^2\to L^2}=o(1) \quad\text{as }j\longrightarrow\infty. \label{n:eq-operator-freeze}$$ For the finitely many smooth reference vectors there is the sharper bound $$\\|(T_j-P_j^*)e_i(s(j+1))\\|_2 \leq C\left(\eta(j)\log\frac1{\eta(j)}+\eta(j)^2\right) =o(\delta_j),\qquad i\leq p. \label{n:eq-frame-freeze}$$ No constant or remainder in these statements depends on the controls at earlier radii. The $o(\delta_j)$ assertion is only on the displayed finite smooth frame, not in the full operator norm.
  - Explanation: Whole-ball harmonic restriction to inner spheres is positive, mean preserving and an L2 contraction for every control sequence, and it is close to the frozen inward maps, with a sharper bound on the finite smooth frame.
- **prop-invariant-exact-graphs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-n-prop-exact-graphs)): After increasing $J$, there is a fixed $\kappa<1$ such that, uniformly in the controls, $$\begin{aligned} T_{QL}&=O(\delta_j),& T_{LL}&=\operatorname{diag}(\alpha_i(j)^{-1})+O(\delta_j),\\ T_{LQ}&=o(1),& \\|T_{QQ}\\|&\leq\kappa\tau_j. \end{aligned} \label{n:eq-transfer-blocks}$$ There is a unique family of graph maps $V_j:\mathbb R^p\to Q_j$, among families in one fixed sufficiently small operator ball, such that $T_j$ maps $$\operatorname{graph}_{j+1}V_{j+1} =\{\iota_{j+1}c+V_{j+1}c:c\in\mathbb R^p\}$$ isomorphically onto $\operatorname{graph}_jV_j$. They satisfy $\\|V_j\\|\leq C\delta_j$. For each fixed $j$, $V_j$ is continuous in operator norm as a function of the complete control sequence with its countable product topology. The outward matrix on these spaces is $$A_j=(T_{LL}+T_{LQ}V_{j+1})^{-1} =\operatorname{diag}(\alpha_i(j))+O(\delta_j), \qquad T_{LQ}V_{j+1}=o(\delta_j). \label{n:eq-exact-outward}$$ It too is continuous in the product topology.
  - Explanation: After a late start, the exact inward maps have block bounds, and there is a unique small family of graph maps over the low angular modes carried isomorphically into each other, depending continuously on the control sequence.
- **prop-signed-off-diagonal-entry** (theorem, [source](#stmt-n-prop-outward-crossing)): Suppose the reference lines $i\neq h$ have an isolated linearly split double crossing at $s_\nu$, with positive eigenvalue $\lambda$. For $L(w)=-\Delta_{h(w)}$, let $$\dot L_{\nu}(s) =\left.\frac{d}{d\epsilon}\right\|_{\epsilon=0} L(w(s)+\epsilon\psi_\nu), \qquad \langle e_h(s_\nu),\dot L_\nu(s_\nu)e_i(s_\nu)\rangle_2\neq0.$$ Write $\eta_\nu=\eta(t_\nu)$, where $s(t_\nu)=s_\nu$. Choose the fixed bump plateau inside a sufficiently small neighborhood of $s_\nu$. For every mesh point whose phase is on that plateau, $$(A_j)_{hi} =z_\nu\eta_\nu^{3/4}m_{hi}(s(j),b(j))+o(\delta_j), \label{n:eq-off-diagonal-outward}$$ uniformly over all controls, past and future. The continuous coefficient $m_{hi}$ has a fixed nonzero sign and a positive lower bound in absolute value on this neighborhood for $b$ near $a$. At the crossing, with $\beta=b^{-2}$ and $\alpha=e^{\mathfrak d(\beta\lambda)}$, it is $$m_{hi}(s_\nu,b) =\alpha\,\beta\,\mathfrak d'(\beta\lambda) \langle e_h(s_\nu),\dot L_\nu(s_\nu)e_i(s_\nu)\rangle_2 . \label{n:eq-crossing-leading-coefficient}$$ The reversed entry has the same nonzero coefficient at the crossing.
  - Explanation: At a split double crossing the off-diagonal entry of the outward matrix is, to leading order, the control times a coefficient of fixed nonzero sign, given explicitly at the crossing.
- **lemma-stable-weights-mismatch-sign** (theorem, [source](#stmt-n-lem-matching-weights)): For all sufficiently late $J$, every trial coordinate defined above satisfies, uniformly on the full product of boxes, $$\|u_{hi}^{\mathrm{trial}}(j)\|\leq C\eta(j)^{1/4} <\eta(j)^{1/8}. \label{n:eq-trial-small}$$ Orient the coordinate $z_\nu$ once according to the sign of its selected coefficient. Each mismatch then satisfies $$D_\nu\|_{z_\nu=1}\geq c_*\eta_\nu^{1/4}>0,\qquad D_\nu\|_{z_\nu=-1}\leq-c_*\eta_\nu^{1/4}<0, \label{n:eq-mismatch-endpoint-signs}$$ independently of every other control and column coordinate.
  - Explanation: Trial coordinates stay strictly inside their boxes, and each scalar mismatch has opposite fixed signs at the two endpoints of its control, independently of all other variables.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-n-prop-angular-program",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $2/3<a<1$, $1<\\vartheta<3a/2$, and $a^2<\\kappa_*<1$. Prescribe a sufficiently small conformal neighborhood $\\mathcal U$ for which the curvature and comparison bounds above hold. For every sufficiently large integer $k$, with threshold allowed to depend on $\\mathcal U$, there are $S>0$ and a smooth $S$-periodic path $w(s)$ in $\\mathcal U$ such that $H(s)=h(w(s))$ has the following properties, with $M,p,L,I,N$ as in <a href=\"#n:eq-band\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"n:eq-band\">[n:eq-band]</a>. The phase origin can be chosen so that $w(s)=w(0)$ for $|s|<\\varepsilon_0$, for some $\\varepsilon_0>0$, and $h(w(0))$ is simple through position $p+1$.*\n\n1.  *The area form is $d\\operatorname{vol}_{H(s)}=4\\pi\\,d\\mu$ pointwise, and the ordered outer gap $\\lambda_p(H(s))<\\lambda_{p+1}(H(s))$ holds for every $s$.*\n\n2.  *Along the unwrapped path $s\\in\\mathbb R$, the spectral subspace of the first $p$ positions has a smooth real $L^2(d\\mu)$-orthonormal frame $e_1(s),\\ldots,e_p(s)$ of continued eigenlines. Their only equalities are isolated double crossings at distinct phases, and at each crossing the traceless first derivative of its $2\\times2$ cluster matrix is nonzero. The return permutation after $S$ fixes positions $1,\\ldots,B_L$ and is one cycle on $I$.*\n\n\n3.  *With $$d(x)=\\frac{-1+\\sqrt{1+4x}}2,$$ every label among the first $p$ satisfies $$\\label{n:eq-frequency-margin}\n           \\frac1{NS}\\int_0^{NS}d\\bigl(a^{-2}\\lambda_i(s)\\bigr)\\,ds<k.$$ Here $NS$ is an eigenvalue period for every band label and also a period for every fixed lower label.*\n\n*At each crossing there is a smooth conformal direction $\\xi$ for which the off-diagonal entry $$\\left\\langle e_h,\n     \\left.\\frac{d}{d\\varepsilon}\\right|_{\\varepsilon=0}\n        (-\\Delta_{h(w+\\varepsilon\\xi)})e_i\n \\right\\rangle_{L^2(d\\mu)}$$ is nonzero on the crossing pair. After $k$ and the entire program are fixed, the outer gap, the absolute crossing slopes, and the separations between crossing phases have positive uniform lower bounds. For every fixed order, the smooth norms of the frame and metrics have a uniform upper bound. For each pair, its absolute eigenvalue gap has a positive lower bound on every compact subset of the full label-period phase circle $\\mathbb R/(NS\\mathbb Z)$ disjoint from that pair’s crossing set. A pair with no crossings has a uniform gap on that whole circle.",
        "source_id": "PM-361.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-n-prop-angular-program",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $2/3<a<1$, $1<\\vartheta<3a/2$, and $a^2<\\kappa_*<1$. Prescribe a sufficiently small conformal neighborhood $\\mathcal U$ for which the curvature and comparison bounds above hold. For every sufficiently large integer $k$, with threshold allowed to depend on $\\mathcal U$, there are $S>0$ and a smooth $S$-periodic path $w(s)$ in $\\mathcal U$ such that $H(s)=h(w(s))$ has the following properties, with $M,p,L,I,N$ as in <a href=\"#n:eq-band\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"n:eq-band\">[n:eq-band]</a>. The phase origin can be chosen so that $w(s)=w(0)$ for $|s|<\\varepsilon_0$, for some $\\varepsilon_0>0$, and $h(w(0))$ is simple through position $p+1$.*\n\n1.  *The area form is $d\\operatorname{vol}_{H(s)}=4\\pi\\,d\\mu$ pointwise, and the ordered outer gap $\\lambda_p(H(s))<\\lambda_{p+1}(H(s))$ holds for every $s$.*\n\n2.  *Along the unwrapped path $s\\in\\mathbb R$, the spectral subspace of the first $p$ positions has a smooth real $L^2(d\\mu)$-orthonormal frame $e_1(s),\\ldots,e_p(s)$ of continued eigenlines. Their only equalities are isolated double crossings at distinct phases, and at each crossing the traceless first derivative of its $2\\times2$ cluster matrix is nonzero. The return permutation after $S$ fixes positions $1,\\ldots,B_L$ and is one cycle on $I$.*\n\n\n3.  *With $$d(x)=\\frac{-1+\\sqrt{1+4x}}2,$$ every label among the first $p$ satisfies $$\\label{n:eq-frequency-margin}\n           \\frac1{NS}\\int_0^{NS}d\\bigl(a^{-2}\\lambda_i(s)\\bigr)\\,ds<k.$$ Here $NS$ is an eigenvalue period for every band label and also a period for every fixed lower label.*\n\n*At each crossing there is a smooth conformal direction $\\xi$ for which the off-diagonal entry $$\\left\\langle e_h,\n     \\left.\\frac{d}{d\\varepsilon}\\right|_{\\varepsilon=0}\n        (-\\Delta_{h(w+\\varepsilon\\xi)})e_i\n \\right\\rangle_{L^2(d\\mu)}$$ is nonzero on the crossing pair. After $k$ and the entire program are fixed, the outer gap, the absolute crossing slopes, and the separations between crossing phases have positive uniform lower bounds. For every fixed order, the smooth norms of the frame and metrics have a uniform upper bound. For each pair, its absolute eigenvalue gap has a positive lower bound on every compact subset of the full label-period phase circle $\\mathbb R/(NS\\mathbb Z)$ disjoint from that pair’s crossing set. A pair with no crossings has a uniform gap on that whole circle.",
          "source_id": "PM-361.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix $2/3<a<1$, $1<\\vartheta<3a/2$, and $a^2<\\kappa_*<1$. The phase origin can be chosen so that $w(s)=w(0)$ for $|s|<\\varepsilon_0$, for some $\\varepsilon_0>0$, and $h(w(0))$ is simple through position $p+1$.*\n\n1.  *The area form is $d\\operatorname{vol}_{H(s)}=4\\pi\\,d\\mu$ pointwise, and the ordered outer gap $\\lambda_p(H(s))<\\lambda_{p+1}(H(s))$ holds for every $s$.*\n\n2.  *Along the unwrapped path $s\\in\\mathbb R$, the spectral subspace of the first $p$ positions has a smooth real $L^2(d\\mu)$-orthonormal frame $e_1(s),\\ldots,e_p(s)$ of continued eigenlines.       "explanation": "A periodic fixed-area path of near-round sphere metrics exists whose continued eigenlines have isolated linearly split double crossings, whose return permutation is a single cycle on the band, and whose every label has mean cone frequency strictly below k.",

      "id": "prop-angular-program",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-n-prop-radial-realization",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose the fixed reference program, including its negative-phase interpolation, is close enough to round that its Gauss curvature is uniformly greater than $\\kappa_*$. There are a constant $C>0$ and, after $C$ is fixed, an integer $J_0$ such that for every integer $J\\geq J_0$ there is a smooth function $f=f_J:[0,\\infty)\\to[0,\\infty)$, independent of $z$, such that $$g_z=dr^2+f(r)^2H_z(\\log r)$$ extends to a complete smooth metric on $\\mathbb R^3$ for every $z\\in[-1,1]^{\\mathbb N}$. The metric is Euclidean near zero, $\\operatorname{Ric}_{g_z}\\geq0$, with strictly positive Ricci curvature outside a compact set, and $d_{g_z}(0,(r,x))=r$. Moreover $$a\\leq f'(r)\\leq1,\\qquad f'(r)\\to a,\\qquad b(t):=e^{-t}f(e^t)\\to a.$$ For every $t\\geq0.8J$ the following estimates are uniform over the entire product box of controls, also after any fixed number of angular differentiations: $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\partial_tH_z=O(\\eta),\\qquad \\partial_t^2H_z=O(\\eta^2),\n   \\qquad \\operatorname{tr}(H_z^{-1}\\partial_tH_z)=0,\n   \\label{n:angular-time-input}\\\\\n &b_{tt}+b_t=-C\\eta^2,\\qquad\n   b_t=O(C\\eta^2),\\quad b_{tt}=O(C\\eta^2),\\quad\n   b-a=O(Ct^{-1/2}). \\label{n:radial-tail-input}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In every fixed smooth angular norm, $H_z(t)-H(s(t))=O(\\eta(t)^{3/4})$ in this range, where the reference $H(s)=h(w(s))$ includes the negative-phase interpolation. The controlled links stay in a fixed compact subset of a finite-dimensional smooth family and have $K_{H_z}>\\kappa_*$. All implied constants may depend on $a$, $\\kappa_*$, the fixed angular program, and the fixed $C$; they do not depend on $J\\geq J_0$ or on the control sequence.",
        "source_id": "PM-361.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

The metric is Euclidean near zero, $\\operatorname{Ric}_{g_z}\\geq0$, with strictly positive Ricci curvature outside a compact set, and $d_{g_z}(0,(r,x))=r$. The controlled links stay in a fixed compact subset of a finite-dimensional smooth family and have $K_{H_z}>\\kappa_*$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-n-lem-inward-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "For every control sequence, $\\mathcal R^z_{r,R}$ is positivity preserving, fixes constants, preserves the $\\mu$-mean, and is a contraction on $L^2(\\mu)$ for every $0<r<R$. In particular these conclusions hold for $T_j$. Uniformly over all controls and all sufficiently late starts, $$\\|T_j-P_j^0\\|_{L^2\\to L^2}=o(1)\n \\quad\\text{as }j\\longrightarrow\\infty.\n \\label{n:eq-operator-freeze}$$ For the finitely many smooth reference vectors there is the sharper bound $$\\|(T_j-P_j^*)e_i(s(j+1))\\|_2\n \\leq C\\left(\\eta(j)\\log\\frac1{\\eta(j)}+\\eta(j)^2\\right)\n =o(\\delta_j),\\qquad i\\leq p.\n \\label{n:eq-frame-freeze}$$ No constant or remainder in these statements depends on the controls at earlier radii. The $o(\\delta_j)$ assertion is only on the displayed finite smooth frame, not in the full operator norm.",
        "source_id": "PM-361.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-n-prop-exact-graphs",
        "exact_quote": "After increasing $J$, there is a fixed $\\kappa<1$ such that, uniformly in the controls, $$\\begin{aligned}\n T_{QL}&=O(\\delta_j),&\n T_{LL}&=\\operatorname{diag}(\\alpha_i(j)^{-1})+O(\\delta_j),\\\\\n T_{LQ}&=o(1),&\n \\|T_{QQ}\\|&\\leq\\kappa\\tau_j.\n \\end{aligned}\n \\label{n:eq-transfer-blocks}$$ There is a unique family of graph maps $V_j:\\mathbb R^p\\to Q_j$, among families in one fixed sufficiently small operator ball, such that $T_j$ maps $$\\operatorname{graph}_{j+1}V_{j+1}\n =\\{\\iota_{j+1}c+V_{j+1}c:c\\in\\mathbb R^p\\}$$ isomorphically onto $\\operatorname{graph}_jV_j$. They satisfy $\\|V_j\\|\\leq C\\delta_j$. For each fixed $j$, $V_j$ is continuous in operator norm as a function of the complete control sequence with its countable product topology. The outward matrix on these spaces is $$A_j=(T_{LL}+T_{LQ}V_{j+1})^{-1}\n     =\\operatorname{diag}(\\alpha_i(j))+O(\\delta_j),\n \\qquad T_{LQ}V_{j+1}=o(\\delta_j).\n \\label{n:eq-exact-outward}$$ It too is continuous in the product topology.",
        "source_id": "PM-361.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-n-prop-exact-graphs",
          "exact_quote": "After increasing $J$, there is a fixed $\\kappa<1$ such that, uniformly in the controls, $$\\begin{aligned}\n T_{QL}&=O(\\delta_j),&\n T_{LL}&=\\operatorname{diag}(\\alpha_i(j)^{-1})+O(\\delta_j),\\\\\n T_{LQ}&=o(1),&\n \\|T_{QQ}\\|&\\leq\\kappa\\tau_j.\n \\end{aligned}\n \\label{n:eq-transfer-blocks}$$ There is a unique family of graph maps $V_j:\\mathbb R^p\\to Q_j$, among families in one fixed sufficiently small operator ball, such that $T_j$ maps $$\\operatorname{graph}_{j+1}V_{j+1}\n =\\{\\iota_{j+1}c+V_{j+1}c:c\\in\\mathbb R^p\\}$$ isomorphically onto $\\operatorname{graph}_jV_j$. They satisfy $\\|V_j\\|\\leq C\\delta_j$. For each fixed $j$, $V_j$ is continuous in operator norm as a function of the complete control sequence with its countable product topology. The outward matrix on these spaces is $$A_j=(T_{LL}+T_{LQ}V_{j+1})^{-1}\n     =\\operatorname{diag}(\\alpha_i(j))+O(\\delta_j),\n \\qquad T_{LQ}V_{j+1}=o(\\delta_j).\n \\label{n:eq-exact-outward}$$ It too is continuous in the product topology.",

        }

      "exact_statement": "After increasing $J$, there is a fixed $\\kappa<1$ such that, uniformly in the controls, $$\\begin{aligned}\n T_{QL}&=O(\\delta_j),&\n T_{LL}&=\\operatorname{diag}(\\alpha_i(j)^{-1})+O(\\delta_j),\\\\\n T_{LQ}&=o(1),&\n \\|T_{QQ}\\|&\\leq\\kappa\\tau_j.\n \\end{aligned}\n \\label{n:eq-transfer-blocks}$$ There is a unique family of graph maps $V_j:\\mathbb R^p\\to Q_j$, among families in one fixed sufficiently small operator ball, such that $T_j$ maps $$\\operatorname{graph}_{j+1}V_{j+1}\n =\\{\\iota_{j+1}c+V_{j+1}c:c\\in\\mathbb R^p\\}$$ isomorphically onto $\\operatorname{graph}_jV_j$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-n-prop-outward-crossing",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose the reference lines $i\\neq h$ have an isolated linearly split double crossing at $s_\\nu$, with positive eigenvalue $\\lambda$. For $L(w)=-\\Delta_{h(w)}$, let $$\\dot L_{\\nu}(s)\n =\\left.\\frac{d}{d\\epsilon}\\right|_{\\epsilon=0}\n       L(w(s)+\\epsilon\\psi_\\nu),\n \\qquad\n \\langle e_h(s_\\nu),\\dot L_\\nu(s_\\nu)e_i(s_\\nu)\\rangle_2\\neq0.$$ Write $\\eta_\\nu=\\eta(t_\\nu)$, where $s(t_\\nu)=s_\\nu$. Choose the fixed bump plateau inside a sufficiently small neighborhood of $s_\\nu$. For every mesh point whose phase is on that plateau, $$(A_j)_{hi}\n =z_\\nu\\eta_\\nu^{3/4}m_{hi}(s(j),b(j))+o(\\delta_j),\n \\label{n:eq-off-diagonal-outward}$$ uniformly over all controls, past and future. The continuous coefficient $m_{hi}$ has a fixed nonzero sign and a positive lower bound in absolute value on this neighborhood for $b$ near $a$. At the crossing, with $\\beta=b^{-2}$ and $\\alpha=e^{\\mathfrak d(\\beta\\lambda)}$, it is $$m_{hi}(s_\\nu,b)\n =\\alpha\\,\\beta\\,\\mathfrak d'(\\beta\\lambda)\n       \\langle e_h(s_\\nu),\\dot L_\\nu(s_\\nu)e_i(s_\\nu)\\rangle_2 .\n \\label{n:eq-crossing-leading-coefficient}$$ The reversed entry has the same nonzero coefficient at the crossing.",
        "source_id": "PM-361.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

Choose the fixed bump plateau inside a sufficiently small neighborhood of $s_\\nu$. The continuous coefficient $m_{hi}$ has a fixed nonzero sign and a positive lower bound in absolute value on this neighborhood for $b$ near $a$. At the crossing, with $\\beta=b^{-2}$ and $\\alpha=e^{\\mathfrak d(\\beta\\lambda)}$, it is $$m_{hi}(s_\\nu,b)\n =\\alpha\\,\\beta\\,\\mathfrak d'(\\beta\\lambda)\n       \\langle e_h(s_\\nu),\\dot L_\\nu(s_\\nu)e_i(s_\\nu)\\rangle_2 .\n \\label{n:eq-crossing-leading-coefficient}$$ The reversed entry has the same nonzero coefficient at the crossing.",
          "source_id": "PM-361.2"

      "exact_statement": "Suppose the reference lines $i\\neq h$ have an isolated linearly split double crossing at $s_\\nu$, with positive eigenvalue $\\lambda$. For every mesh point whose phase is on that plateau, $$(A_j)_{hi}\n =z_\\nu\\eta_\\nu^{3/4}m_{hi}(s(j),b(j))+o(\\delta_j),\n \\label{n:eq-off-diagonal-outward}$$ uniformly over all controls, past and future. The continuous coefficient $m_{hi}$ has a fixed nonzero sign and a positive lower bound in absolute value on this neighborhood for $b$ near $a$. At the crossing, with $\\beta=b^{-2}$ and $\\alpha=e^{\\mathfrak d(\\beta\\lambda)}$, it is $$m_{hi}(s_\\nu,b)\n =\\alpha\\,\\beta\\,\\mathfrak d'(\\beta\\lambda)\n       \\langle e_h(s_\\nu),\\dot L_\\nu(s_\\nu)e_i(s_\\nu)\\rangle_2 .\n \\label{n:eq-crossing-leading-coefficient}$$ The reversed entry has the same nonzero coefficient at the crossing.",
      "explanation": "At a split double crossing the off-diagonal entry of the outward matrix is, to leading order, the control times a coefficient of fixed nonzero sign, given explicitly at the crossing.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-n-prop-outward-crossing",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose the reference lines $i\\neq h$ have an isolated linearly split double crossing at $s_\\nu$, with positive eigenvalue $\\lambda$.",
          "source_id": "PM-361.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "prop-signed-off-diagonal-entry",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-n-prop-outward-crossing",
          "exact_quote": "For every mesh point whose phase is on that plateau",
          "source_id": "PM-361.2"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-n-prop-outward-crossing"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-n-lem-matching-weights",
        "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently late $J$, every trial coordinate defined above satisfies, uniformly on the full product of boxes, $$|u_{hi}^{\\mathrm{trial}}(j)|\\leq C\\eta(j)^{1/4}\n       <\\eta(j)^{1/8}.\n \\label{n:eq-trial-small}$$ Orient the coordinate $z_\\nu$ once according to the sign of its selected coefficient. Each mismatch then satisfies $$D_\\nu|_{z_\\nu=1}\\geq c_*\\eta_\\nu^{1/4}>0,\\qquad\n D_\\nu|_{z_\\nu=-1}\\leq-c_*\\eta_\\nu^{1/4}<0,\n \\label{n:eq-mismatch-endpoint-signs}$$ independently of every other control and column coordinate.",
        "source_id": "PM-361.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

Each mismatch then satisfies $$D_\\nu|_{z_\\nu=1}\\geq c_*\\eta_\\nu^{1/4}>0,\\qquad\n D_\\nu|_{z_\\nu=-1}\\leq-c_*\\eta_\\nu^{1/4}<0,\n \\label{n:eq-mismatch-endpoint-signs}$$ independently of every other control and column coordinate.",
          "source_id": "PM-361.2"

Each mismatch then satisfies $$D_\\nu|_{z_\\nu=1}\\geq c_*\\eta_\\nu^{1/4}>0,\\qquad\n D_\\nu|_{z_\\nu=-1}\\leq-c_*\\eta_\\nu^{1/4}<0,\n \\label{n:eq-mismatch-endpoint-signs}$$ independently of every other control and column coordinate.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **harmonic growth space H_d(M,g)** ([source](#n:euclidean-count)): For $d\geq0$, write $\mathcal H_d(M,g)$ for the real vector space of smooth harmonic functions such that $$\|u(x)\|\leq C_u(1+d_g(o,x))^d\qquad(x\in M)$$ with some finite constant $C_u$ depending on $u$.
- **harmonic dimension h_d(M,g)** ([source](#n:euclidean-count)): Let $h_d(M,g)$ denote its real dimension.
- **pointed tangent cone at infinity** ([source](#n:euclidean-count)): A pointed tangent cone at infinity is a pointed Gromov–Hausdorff limit of rescalings $(M,R_j^{-2}g,o)$ with $R_j\to\infty$.
- **round eigenspace Y_l** ([source](#n:eq-round-blocks)): The corresponding real eigenspace is denoted by $\mathcal Y_l$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-two-directions** [claim](#stmt-n-lem-two-directions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-lem-two-directions): Differentiate the generalized eigenvalue equation to get the cluster matrix, and show the two product functions are independent by unique continuation. -> rank-two traceless splitting
- **step-avoidance** [claim](#stmt-n-lem-avoidance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-lem-avoidance): Finite pool of directions, codimension-two zero sets in chart neighborhoods, null projections and countable union. -> dense simple parameters and simple paths
- **step-retained-double** [claim](#stmt-n-lem-retained-double) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-lem-retained-double): Show a double-preserving direction splits any other cluster, since equal nodal sets would force equal eigenvalues, then split repeatedly. -> isolated double kept, others split
- **step-pole-modes** [claim](#stmt-n-lem-pole-modes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-lem-pole-modes): Wronskian argument with pole behavior gives simplicity; absolute value of a minimizer gives positivity; continuity fixes parity. -> simple sector eigenvalues with constant parity
- **step-adjacent-doubles** [claim](#stmt-n-lem-adjacent-doubles) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-lem-adjacent-doubles): Inside a block use the symmetric-square product isomorphism and a submersion; across blocks use a parity inversion in an even path and continuity. -> isolated double at each adjacent band position
- **step-angular-program** [claim](#stmt-n-prop-angular-program) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-prop-angular-program): Join short crossing loops through a simple base point, concatenate them into one cycle, and average the round-block frequency bound over band ranks. -> periodic program with mean frequency margin
- **step-radial-realization** [claim](#stmt-n-prop-radial-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-prop-radial-realization): Concave tail for the warping function and Schur complement of the Ricci blocks, with a tail constant chosen before the late start. -> complete metric with nonnegative Ricci curvature, uniform estimates
- **step-inward-transfer** [claim](#stmt-n-lem-inward-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-lem-inward-transfer): Maximum principle for positivity and contraction, then compactness for the operator freeze and a barrier comparison for the finite frame. -> contractive whole-ball restriction close to frozen maps
- **step-exact-graphs** [claim](#stmt-n-prop-exact-graphs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-prop-exact-graphs): Block estimates from the frozen comparisons and a contraction mapping inward for the graph maps, with continuity in the product topology. -> invariant graphs and outward matrices
- **step-outward-crossing** [claim](#stmt-n-prop-outward-crossing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-prop-outward-crossing): Differentiate the compressed frozen inward map in the reference eigenbasis using divided differences and invert. -> signed first-order off-diagonal coefficient
- **step-matching-weights** [claim](#stmt-n-lem-matching-weights) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-lem-matching-weights): Gaussian bounds on stable products near simple crossings and a central-window argument for the sign of the mismatch. -> bounded trials and endpoint signs of mismatches
- **step-simultaneous-fixed-point** [claim](#stmt-n-lem-matching-weights) / [proof](#pm-h-22): Finite-dimensional Brouwer on truncations and a diagonal limit select all crossing controls at once. -> exactly invariant lines for one metric
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-n-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-lem-matching-weights-2): Lift the invariant lines to entire harmonic functions, convert the phase average to a pointwise degree-k bound, and fix the order of parameter choices. -> complete metric with harmonic dimension above the Euclidean count
- **step-near-euclidean** [claim](#stmt-n-near-euclidean) / [proof](#proof-stmt-n-lem-matching-weights-3): Choose the warping parameter and a small conformal neighborhood so the global tensor comparison gives the bi-Lipschitz bounds. -> near-Euclidean metrics with the same conclusions

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-excess-family**: For each admissible volume ratio v and excess factor c, every sufficiently large degree k admits a complete smooth metric on R^3, Euclidean near the origin, with nonnegative Ricci curvature, asymptotic volume ratio v, and at least c(k+1)^2 independent harmonic functions of growth at most k.
- **inv-near-euclidean**: For every positive epsilon and every excess factor in the stated range, large degrees admit metrics with the conclusions of the main theorem that are globally bi-Lipschitz close to the Euclidean metric.
- **inv-metric-depends-on-degree**: The metric is selected after the degree is fixed, so the result describes a family of metrics indexed by the degree.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-single-fixed-manifold**: Read the theorem as a harmonic-dimension excess on one fixed manifold for all large degrees. -> The metric changes with the degree, so no single manifold is shown to have the excess at every large degree.
- **anti-nonneg-sectional**: Assume the constructed metrics have nonnegative sectional curvature and import comparison theorems requiring it. -> The metrics have radial planes of negative sectional curvature at points going to infinity, so such hypotheses fail.
- **anti-small-degree**: Apply the excess to every degree, including small ones. -> The statement only gives the excess above a threshold degree depending on v and c.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are existence theorems for metrics on R^3: the control sequence is selected by a Brouwer fixed point on a countable product of intervals followed by a diagonal limit, and the metric is a smooth object defined at all radii. No finite procedure with explicit steps computes the stated objects, so no finite executable oracle decides the claims.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The theorem is an existence result for smooth Riemannian metrics on R^3 with no finite procedure that could be coded as a library routine. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result concerns harmonic functions on a continuous nonnegative-Ricci manifold, with no simulation scheme or discretisation in the paper. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No tool, solver or checker is specified. The only practical use is as a negative test fixture for conjectures, not as a tool. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proof is existential: controls come from a Brouwer fixed point on a countable product followed by a diagonal limit, so no terminating algorithm with a complexity is given. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No data structure appears. The objects are Riemannian metrics, spectral projections and invariant graph maps in Hilbert spaces. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper contains no arithmetic kernel or computation that could be mapped to hardware. Its estimates are asymptotic and hold only for the fixed finite program. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper concerns hardness assumptions, protocols or security notions. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No learning setting, loss or data is present. The only link is the Dirichlet-extension and Laplace-spectrum viewpoint, which is not developed here. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no objective or feasible set. Controls are chosen by a clamped fixed-point map to force mismatches to zero, which is not an optimisation problem. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No processes, messages or consistency notions appear. The recursion over radii is a deterministic difference equation, not a protocol. |
| other | True | moderate | inspirational | inferred | The result is a calibration counterexample for geometric analysis: it shows that Yau's integer-degree comparison fails in dimension three under Ric nonnegative, and it can serve as a conceptual guard for formalisation efforts. It is not a computation. |

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For every $`4/9\lt v\lt 1`$ and $`1\lt c\lt 9v/4`$, all sufficiently large integers <i>k</i> admit a complete smooth metric on ℝ<sup>3</sup> with nonnegative Ricci curvature, asymptotic volume ratio <i>v</i>, and at least $`c(k+1)^2`$ linearly independent real harmonic functions of pointwise growth at most <i>k</i>. This answers Yau's integer-degree dimension comparison question negatively in dimension three, with a fixed-factor excess over the Euclidean count. For each $`1\lt c\lt 9/4`$, these metrics can be chosen arbitrarily close to the Euclidean metric in global bi-Lipschitz distance. The metric may depend on <i>k</i>.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
